float s13110(int iterations,float aa[256][256])
{
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        max = aa[(0)][0];
        xindex = 0;
        yindex = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] > max) {
                    max = aa[i][j];
                    xindex = i;
                    yindex = j;
                }
            }
        }
        chksum = max + (float) xindex + (float) yindex;
    }
    return max + xindex+1 + yindex+1;
}
```

## 2. Semantic comparison with simple test cases

Source code logic:  
- Outer loop `nl` runs `100 * (iterations / 256)` times.  
- Each iteration:  
  1. Find `max` in `aa[0..255][0..255]` and its `(xindex, yindex)`.  
  2. Compute `chksum = max + xindex + yindex` (unused except in loop).  
- After loop ends, return `max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1`.  
  Wait — careful: `return max + xindex+1 + yindex+1` means `max + (xindex+1) + (yindex+1)`? No — C operator precedence: `+` is left-associative, `max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1` is `(((max + xindex) + 1) + yindex) + 1`.  
  So final return = `max + xindex + yindex + 2`.

But note: `max`, `xindex`, `yindex` after the loop are from the **last iteration** of the outer loop.  
In the last iteration, `max` is the maximum of `aa` at that time (same array every iteration, so same max each time unless array changes between calls — but here `aa` is unchanged inside the function).  
So `max`, `xindex`, `yindex` are the same every outer iteration.  
Thus final result = `max_aa + xindex_max + yindex_max + 2`.

---

Suppose `aa` is 2x2 for simplicity:  
aa[0][0]=1, aa[0][1]=2, aa[1][0]=3, aa[1][1]=4.  
Max = 4 at (1,1).

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

```c
float s13110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    int outer_iters = 100 * (iterations / 256);
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < outer_iters; nl++) {
        max = aa[0][0];
        xindex = 0;
        yindex = 0;
        
        float row_max[256];
        int row_xindex[256];
        int row_yindex[256];
        
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float local_max = aa[i][0];
            int local_yidx = 0;
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float val = aa[i][j];
                if (val > local_max) {
                    local_max = val;
                    local_yidx = j;
                }
            }
            row_max[i] = local_max;
            row_xindex[i] = i;
            row_yindex[i] = local_yidx;
        }
        
        float final_max = row_max[0];
        int final_xidx = row_xindex[0];
        int final_yidx = row_yindex[0];
        
        for (int i = 1; i < 256; i++) {
            if (row_max[i] > final_max) {
                final_max = row_max[i];
                final_xidx = row_xindex[i];
                final_yidx = row_yindex[i];
            }
        }
        
        max = final_max;
        xindex = final_xidx;
        yindex = final_yidx;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

Optimize code:

float s13110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    int outer_iters = 100 * (iterations / 256);
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < outer_iters; nl++) {
        max = aa[0][0];
        xindex = 0;
        yindex = 0;
        
        float row_max[256];
        int row_xindex[256];
        int row_yindex[256];
        
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float local_max = aa[i][0];
            int local_yidx = 0;
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float val = aa[i][j];
                if (val > local_max) {
                    local_max = val;
                    local_yidx = j;
                }
            }
            row_max[i] = local_max;
            row_xindex[i] = i;
            row_yindex[i] = local_yidx;
        }
        
        float final_max = row_max[0];
        int final_xidx = row_xindex[0];
        int final_yidx = row_yindex[0];
        
        for (int i = 1; i < 256; i++) {
            if (row_max[i] > final_max) {
                final_max = row_max[i];
                final_xidx = row_xindex[i];
                final_yidx = row_yindex[i];
            }
        }
        
        max = final_max;
        xindex = final_xidx;
        yindex = final_yidx;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

```

## FEEDBACK OUTPUT
## Analysis  

### 1. Compilation Errors in Optimized Code  
The optimized code compiles without syntax errors, so we proceed.  

### 2. Semantic Comparison with Simple Test Cases  

The original code:  
- For each outer iteration `nl`, finds the **global maximum** in `aa[256][256]` and its `(xindex, yindex)`.  
- Computes `chksum = max + xindex + yindex` (but `chksum` is unused later).  
- Returns `max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1`.  

The optimized code:  
- Splits the search into two phases:  
  1. For each row `i`, find the maximum in that row (`local_max`) and its column index (`local_yidx`).  
  2. Then find the maximum among `row_max[i]` across rows, tracking the corresponding `(row_xindex[i], row_yindex[i])`.  

This is **semantically correct** because:  
- The row-wise max step finds the maximum in each row and its column index.  
- The second step finds the global maximum across rows, and the stored `(row_xindex[i], row_yindex[i])` gives the correct position.  

**Example verification**:  
Let `aa[2][2]` = {{1.0, 3.0}, {2.0, 0.5}}.  
Original: max = 3.0 at (0,1) → returns 3.0 + 0+1 + 1+1 = 6.0.  
Optimized: Row 0 max=3.0 at (0,1), Row 1 max=2.0 at (1,0) → final max=3.0 at (0,1) → same result.  

Thus, semantics match.  

### 3. Vectorization Analysis  

Compiler feedback shows:  
- Inner row loop (`for j`) not vectorized because `local_max` and `local_yidx` are updated conditionally (reduction-like but with index tracking).  
- Outer reduction loop (`for i` over rows) not vectorized because `final_max`, `final_xidx`, `final_yidx` are updated conditionally (also reduction with index).  

**Why not auto-vectorized?**  
- Vectorization of max-with-index reductions is nontrivial:  
  - The max value reduction is vectorizable (using `vmaxps`), but tracking the index requires comparing vector elements and blending indices.  
  - Compiler cannot prove safe vectorization here due to the index updates being conditional on the max comparison.  

**Optimization opportunity**:  
The current optimization already improves cache locality (row-wise access), but the inner loop still has a dependency chain (`local_max`, `local_yidx`).  
We could:  
1. Use SIMD explicitly for max-with-index (e.g., vectorize over 8 floats, track max per lane, reduce at end).  
2. Or, since the compiler can’t auto-vectorize, we could restructure to help it:  
   - For the inner row max: compute max value via vectorization (without index), then find index in a second pass (less efficient).  
   - For the outer reduction: same issue.  

Given the compiler feedback says “not vectorized,” there is **still optimization opportunity** (we could manually vectorize or restructure to allow auto-vectorization).  

### 4. Conclusion  

Since the code is **not fully optimized** (vectorization possible with manual changes), and the unit test passed but vectorization failed, we output:  

**FAIL**

float s13110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    int outer_iters = 100 * (iterations / 256);
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < outer_iters; nl++) {
        max = aa[0][0];
        xindex = 0;
        yindex = 0;
        
        float row_max[256];
        int row_xindex[256];
        int row_yindex[256];
        
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float local_max = aa[i][0];
            int local_yidx = 0;
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float val = aa[i][j];
                if (val > local_max) {
                    local_max = val;
                    local_yidx = j;
                }
            }
            row_max[i] = local_max;
            row_xindex[i] = i;
            row_yindex[i] = local_yidx;
        }
        
        float final_max = row_max[0];
        int final_xidx = row_xindex[0];
        int final_yidx = row_yindex[0];
        
        for (int i = 1; i < 256; i++) {
            if (row_max[i] > final_max) {
                final_max = row_max[i];
                final_xidx = row_xindex[i];
                final_yidx = row_yindex[i];
            }
        }
        
        max = final_max;
        xindex = final_xidx;
        yindex = final_yidx;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

```

## Analysis  

### 1. Compilation Errors in Optimized Code  
The optimized code compiles without syntax errors, so we proceed.  

### 2. Semantic Comparison with Simple Test Cases  

The original code:  
- For each outer iteration `nl`, finds the **global maximum** in `aa[256][256]` and its `(xindex, yindex)`.  
- Computes `chksum = max + xindex + yindex` (but `chksum` is unused later).  
- Returns `max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1`.  

The optimized code:  
- Splits the search into two phases:  
  1. For each row `i`, find the maximum in that row (`local_max`) and its column index (`local_yidx`).  
  2. Then find the maximum among `row_max[i]` across rows, tracking the corresponding `(row_xindex[i], row_yindex[i])`.  

This is **semantically correct** because:  
- The row-wise max step finds the maximum in each row and its column index.  
- The second step finds the global maximum across rows, and the stored `(row_xindex[i], row_yindex[i])` gives the correct position.  

**Example verification**:  
Let `aa[2][2]` = {{1.0, 3.0}, {2.0, 0.5}}.  
Original: max = 3.0 at (0,1) → returns 3.0 + 0+1 + 1+1 = 6.0.  
Optimized: Row 0 max=3.0 at (0,1), Row 1 max=2.0 at (1,0) → final max=3.0 at (0,1) → same result.  

Thus, semantics match.  

### 3. Vectorization Analysis  

Compiler feedback shows:  
- Inner row loop (`for j`) not vectorized because `local_max` and `local_yidx` are updated conditionally (reduction-like but with index tracking).  
- Outer reduction loop (`for i` over rows) not vectorized because `final_max`, `final_xidx`, `final_yidx` are updated conditionally (also reduction with index).  

**Why not auto-vectorized?**  
- Vectorization of max-with-index reductions is nontrivial:  
  - The max value reduction is vectorizable (using `vmaxps`), but tracking the index requires comparing vector elements and blending indices.  
  - Compiler cannot prove safe vectorization here due to the index updates being conditional on the max comparison.  

**Optimization opportunity**:  
The current optimization already improves cache locality (row-wise access), but the inner loop still has a dependency chain (`local_max`, `local_yidx`).  
We could:  
1. Use SIMD explicitly for max-with-index (e.g., vectorize over 8 floats, track max per lane, reduce at end).  
2. Or, since the compiler can’t auto-vectorize, we could restructure to help it:  
   - For the inner row max: compute max value via vectorization (without index), then find index in a second pass (less efficient).  
   - For the outer reduction: same issue.  

Given the compiler feedback says “not vectorized,” there is **still optimization opportunity** (we could manually vectorize or restructure to allow auto-vectorization).

## Analysis  

### 1. Compilation errors  
The optimized code compiles without errors (the compiler remarks are warnings, not errors).  

### 2. Semantic comparison  
The optimized code changes the algorithm:  
- Original: Scans all `aa[i][j]` in row-major order, tracking the **first occurrence** of the maximum value (since `>` only updates when strictly greater, earlier equal values keep earlier indices).  
- Optimized: Finds the **per-row maximum** and its column index, then finds the maximum across rows. This can change `(xindex, yindex)` when there are multiple equal maxima, because:  
  - Within a row, the inner loop starts from `j = 1`, so if `aa[i][0]` equals the row max, `local_yidx` stays 0 (correct for first occurrence in that row).  
  - Across rows, if two rows have the same max value, the second loop picks the **first row** (since `row_max[i] > final_max` uses `>` not `>=`), which matches original row-major order for the first row’s max.  
  - However, within that winning row, the column index is the **first occurrence in that row** (starting from j=0), which matches original semantics.  

Thus, the optimized code **preserves** the “first occurrence” semantics in row-major order.  
Unit test passes confirm this.  

### 3. Vectorization analysis  
Compiler feedback:  
- Inner loop (`for j`) not vectorized because `local_max` and `local_yidx` are updated conditionally, and the compiler cannot prove they are reductions of a form it can vectorize (due to the index tracking).  
- Outer reduction loop (`for i`) not vectorized because `final_max`, `final_xidx`, `final_yidx` are also conditionally updated with index tracking.  

**Why not vectorizable automatically?**  
Tracking both value and index requires a **max-value reduction with argmax**, which is not a supported reduction pattern in standard auto-vectorization. The conditional selection of indices breaks SIMD parallel reduction.  

**Optimization opportunity:**  
We could restructure to separate the max-finding from index-finding:  
1. Find max value via vectorized max reduction (no index tracking).  
2. In a second pass, find first occurrence of that max in row-major order.  
But this changes performance characteristics (two passes).  

Given the current optimized code still has **vectorization blockers**, there is **optimization opportunity** (the compiler cannot vectorize it).  

### 4. Conclusion  
The optimized code is semantically correct but **not fully optimized** for vectorization. Since there is clear optimization opportunity (loop not vectorized due to argmax pattern), this does not meet the “fully optimized, no further modification needed” requirement.

     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

float s13110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    int outer_iters = 100 * (iterations / 256);
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < outer_iters; nl++) {
        float row_max[256];
        int row_yindex[256];
        
        // First pass: find max in each row (vectorizable)
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float local_max = aa[i][0];
            int local_yidx = 0;
            float current_val;
            
            // Unroll first few iterations to help vectorization
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                current_val = aa[i][j];
                // Branchless conditional update
                int cond = current_val > local_max;
                local_max = cond ? current_val : local_max;
                local_yidx = cond ? j : local_yidx;
            }
            
            row_max[i] = local_max;
            row_yindex[i] = local_yidx;
        }
        
        // Second pass: find max across rows (vectorizable)
        float final_max = row_max[0];
        int final_xidx = 0;
        int final_yidx = row_yindex[0];
        
        for (int i = 1; i < 256; i++) {
            // Branchless conditional update
            int cond = row_max[i] > final_max;
            final_max = cond ? row_max[i] : final_max;
            final_xidx = cond ? i : final_xidx;
            final_yidx = cond ? row_yindex[i] : final_yidx;
        }
        
        max = final_max;
        xindex = final_xidx;
        yindex = final_yidx;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

Across rows: max=5, xidx=0, yidx=0 → same result.

Example 3:  
`aa = [[1, 3], [3, 1]]`  
Source: max=3, first occurrence at (0,1) → xindex=0, yindex=1 → return `3 + 0+1 + 1+1 = 6`.  
Optimized:  
Row 0: max=3, yidx=1  
Row 1: max=3, yidx=0  
Across rows: max=3, xidx=0, yidx=1 → same result.

Compiler feedback says:  
- Inner loop `for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++)` not vectorized because “value that could not be identified as reduction is used outside the loop” — here `local_max` and `local_yidx` are reductions, but `local_yidx` is an index, not a simple arithmetic reduction; compilers often struggle to vectorize max-with-index patterns because the index update is conditional on a comparison across lanes.  
- Same for the second pass across rows.

**Why not vectorized automatically:**  
- Finding max value is a reduction (vectorizable with `max` operation).  
- Finding index of max requires tracking both value and index, which involves horizontal operations across vector lanes that break simple SIMD pattern without special instructions (like `_mm_max_ps` with index tracking).  

**Optimization opportunity:**  
We can separate the max-finding from index-finding to help vectorization:  
1. Vectorize max per row using SIMD max operations.  
2. After finding max per row, do a single pass to find its index (scalar).  
This reduces work but still requires scalar for index. However, the compiler might vectorize the max reduction part if we write it as a plain max loop without index tracking inside the vectorized part.

Current code mixes max and index update in same loop — prevents vectorization.  
We can restructure:  
First loop: compute `row_max[i]` (vectorizable max reduction).  
Second loop: find column index for that row by scanning again (or scanning only if needed — but scanning again is extra work).  
But scanning again doubles row work. Better: keep per-row max vectorized, index finding scalar in separate pass per row — but that’s still not fully vectorized for index.

Given the compiler says not vectorized, there is an optimization opportunity (splitting max and index finding), so we should output **FAIL**.

Since vectorization is possible with code changes (splitting max reduction from index reduction), and current version is not vectorized, optimization opportunity exists → **FAIL**.

Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)

     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```

   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops

float s13110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    int outer_iters = 100 * (iterations / 256);
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < outer_iters; nl++) {
        float row_max[256];
        int row_yindex[256];
        
        // First pass: find max in each row (vectorizable max reduction)
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float local_max = aa[i][0];
            // Vectorizable max reduction loop
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current_val = aa[i][j];
                local_max = (current_val > local_max) ? current_val : local_max;
            }
            row_max[i] = local_max;
        }
        
        // Second pass: find column index for each row's max
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            int local_yidx = 0;
            float target_max = row_max[i];
            // Scan to find first occurrence of max
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] == target_max) {
                    local_yidx = j;
                    break;
                }
            }
            row_yindex[i] = local_yidx;
        }
        
        // Third pass: find max across rows (vectorizable reduction)
        float final_max = row_max[0];
        int final_xidx = 0;
        for (int i = 1; i < 256; i++) {
            final_max = (row_max[i] > final_max) ? row_max[i] : final_max;
        }
        
        // Fourth pass: find row index of final_max
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            if (row_max[i] == final_max) {
                final_xidx = i;
                break;
            }
        }
        
        max = final_max;

Optimize code:
```c

float s13110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    int outer_iters = 100 * (iterations / 256);
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < outer_iters; nl++) {
        float row_max[256];
        int row_yindex[256];
        
        // First pass: find max in each row (vectorizable max reduction)
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float local_max = aa[i][0];
            // Vectorizable max reduction loop
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current_val = aa[i][j];
                local_max = (current_val > local_max) ? current_val : local_max;
            }
            row_max[i] = local_max;
        }
        
        // Second pass: find column index for each row's max
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            int local_yidx = 0;
            float target_max = row_max[i];
            // Scan to find first occurrence of max
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] == target_max) {
                    local_yidx = j;
                    break;
                }
            }
            row_yindex[i] = local_yidx;
        }
        
        // Third pass: find max across rows (vectorizable reduction)
        float final_max = row_max[0];
        int final_xidx = 0;
        for (int i = 1; i < 256; i++) {
            final_max = (row_max[i] > final_max) ? row_max[i] : final_max;
        }
        
        // Fourth pass: find row index of final_max
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            if (row_max[i] == final_max) {
                final_xidx = i;
                break;
            }
        }
        
        max = final_max;
        xindex = final_xidx;
        yindex = row_yindex[final_xidx];
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

```

UNIT TEST PASS

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;

float s13110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;

        float row_max[256];
        int row_yindex[256];
        
        // First pass: find max in each row (fully vectorizable)
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float local_max = aa[i][0];
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current_val = aa[i][j];
                local_max = (current_val > local_max) ? current_val : local_max;
            }
            row_max[i] = local_max;
        }
        
        // Second pass: find column index for each row's max
        // Remove early exit to enable vectorization
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            int local_yidx = 0;
            float target_max = row_max[i];
            // Scan entire row, keep last occurrence
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                // Use comparison without break - enables vectorization
                local_yidx = (aa[i][j] == target_max) ? j : local_yidx;
            }
            row_yindex[i] = local_yidx;
        }
        
        // Third pass: find max across rows (vectorizable reduction)
        float final_max = row_max[0];
        for (int i = 1; i < 256; i++) {
            final_max = (row_max[i] > final_max) ? row_max[i] : final_max;
        }
        
        // Fourth pass: find row index of final_max
        // Remove early exit to enable vectorization
        int final_xidx = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            // Keep last occurrence matching source semantics
            final_xidx = (row_max[i] == final_max) ? i : final_xidx;
        }
        
        max = final_max;
        xindex = final_xidx;
        yindex = row_yindex[final_xidx];
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

## Analysis Report

### 1. Compilation Errors in Optimized Code
The optimized code compiles successfully (no compilation errors reported). The compiler feedback shows only vectorization remarks, not errors.

### 2. Semantic Comparison with Simple Test Cases

**Critical Issue Found**: The optimized code has different semantics from the source code.

**Problem Analysis**:
1. **Source code behavior**: Finds the **first occurrence** of the maximum value (when scanning row-major order)
2. **Optimized code behavior**: Finds the **last occurrence** of the maximum value (due to `local_yidx = (aa[i][j] == target_max) ? j : local_yidx` and similar logic for `final_xidx`)

**Example Verification**:
Consider a 2x2 matrix for simplicity:
```
aa[0][0] = 5.0, aa[0][1] = 5.0  (both max values)
aa[1][0] = 3.0, aa[1][1] = 4.0
```

**Source code execution**:
- Scans row-major: (0,0), (0,1), (1,0), (1,1)
- Finds max=5.0 at (0,0) first
- Returns: max=5.0, xindex=0, yindex=0

**Optimized code execution**:
- Row 0: finds max=5.0, stores last occurrence at (0,1)
- Row 1: finds max=4.0
- Final max=5.0, finds last row with max=5.0 (row 0)
- Returns: max=5.0, xindex=0, yindex=1 (WRONG!)

**Dependencies**: The optimization introduces false dependencies by changing the scanning order semantics. The source code has a true dependency on the order of maximum detection (first occurrence matters).

**Conclusion**: The optimized code does not preserve the original semantics. The unit test may have passed due to test cases where maximum values are unique, but the behavior differs when there are duplicate maximum values.

### 3. Vectorization Analysis (Not Applicable Due to Semantic Issues)

Since the semantics are incorrect, vectorization analysis is not meaningful at this stage. The compiler feedback shows some loops are vectorized, but the fundamental algorithm is wrong.

### Final Assessment: FAIL

The optimized code fails the semantic comparison test. The key issue is that it finds the last occurrence of the maximum value instead of the first occurrence as in the original code. This changes the output when there are duplicate maximum values in the array.

UNIT TEST PASS

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

## Analysis Report  

### 1. Compilation Errors in Optimized Code  
The optimized code compiles without errors.  

### 2. Semantic Comparison with Simple Test Cases  
The optimized code **does not preserve the original semantics**.  

**Reason:**  
- In the original code, when multiple elements have the same maximum value, the algorithm picks the first occurrence (lowest `i`, then lowest `j`) because it scans row‑major and updates `xindex`/`yindex` only when `aa[i][j] > max`.  
- In the optimized code:  
  1. The first pass finds the maximum value in each row (`row_max[i]`).  
  2. The second pass finds the **first column index** where `aa[i][j] == row_max[i]`.  
  3. The third pass finds the maximum across rows (`final_max`).  
  4. The fourth pass finds the **first row index** where `row_max[i] == final_max`.  

**Problem:**  
If the same maximum value appears in multiple rows, the row chosen is the **first row** where `row_max[i] == final_max`, which matches the original. However, the column index is taken from `row_yindex[final_xidx]`, which is the **first occurrence in that row**. This matches the original row‑major scan behavior **only if** the first global maximum occurs in that row.  

But there is a subtle issue:  
- The original code updates `xindex` and `yindex` only when a **strictly greater** value is found.  
- The optimized code uses equality checks (`aa[i][j] == target_max`) to locate the column index. This works because `target_max` is the maximum of that row, so equality implies it’s the maximum.  

However, the **order of discovery** in the original is row‑major, and the optimized code preserves that per row, but across rows it still picks the first row with the global max. This is correct.  

**But** the unit test indicates the output is inconsistent, meaning there is a semantic mismatch. Likely the mismatch arises because the original code’s `chksum` uses `xindex` and `yindex` from the **last iteration** of the outer loop, and the optimized code’s `chksum` uses the same variables, but the inner loops may produce different `xindex`/`yindex` for **tied maxima** in some cases.  

Given the unit test says **UNIT TEST PASS**, the semantic mismatch might be negligible for the test cases, but the compiler feedback shows vectorization issues, so we proceed.  

### 3. Vectorization Analysis (Optimized Code Only)  
Compiler feedback shows:  
- Loop at line 19 (first pass, inner `j` loop) is vectorized.  
- Loop at line 32 (second pass, inner `j` loop) is **not vectorized** because the value (`local_yidx`) cannot be identified as a reduction and is used outside the loop.  
- Loop at line 42 (third pass) is vectorized.  
- Loop at line 48 (fourth pass) is **not vectorized** for the same reason as the second pass.  

**Why vectorization fails for the second and fourth passes:**  
- These loops search for the **first occurrence** of a value, which is not a reduction pattern. The loop carries a dependency: `local_yidx` is conditionally updated based on a “found” condition, and the loop must stop scanning after the first match in the original semantics, but here it scans all elements. Still, the conditional update prevents vectorization because the compiler cannot guarantee the order of updates in vectorized execution.  

**Optimization opportunity:**  
The current code scans rows twice (once for max, once for column index). This can be merged into a single row scan that finds both max and its first column index, but that would reintroduce the same vectorization problem. However, the **first‑occurrence search** is inherently sequential and not vectorizable without significant changes (e.g., using parallel reduction on index with max‑value tie‑breaking).  

Given the compiler remarks indicate **non‑vectorized loops**, and the unit test passes, the optimized code is **not fully optimized**—there are still loops that could be improved for vectorization.

The optimized code compiles and passes unit tests but contains non‑vectorized loops, indicating further optimization is possible. Therefore, the result is:

```c
float s13110(int iterations,float aa[256][256])
{
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        max = aa[(0)][0];
        xindex = 0;
        yindex = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] > max) {
                    max = aa[i][j];
                    xindex = i;
                    yindex = j;
                }
            }
        }
        chksum = max + (float) xindex + (float) yindex;
    }
    return max + xindex+1 + yindex+1;
}
```

Optimized code:
```c

float s13110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    int outer_iters = 100 * (iterations / 256);
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < outer_iters; nl++) {
        float row_max[256];
        int row_yindex[256];
        
        // First pass: find max in each row (fully vectorizable reduction)
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float local_max = aa[i][0];
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current_val = aa[i][j];
                local_max = (current_val > local_max) ? current_val : local_max;
            }
            row_max[i] = local_max;
        }
        
        // Second pass: find first column index for each row's max
        // Use explicit comparison without early exit for vectorization
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            int local_yidx = 0;
            float target_max = row_max[i];
            // Find first occurrence by scanning forward
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                // Use comparison that preserves first occurrence
                int found = (aa[i][j] == target_max) && (local_yidx == 0);
                local_yidx = found ? j : local_yidx;
            }
            row_yindex[i] = local_yidx;
        }
        
        // Third pass: find max across rows (vectorizable reduction)
        float final_max = row_max[0];
        for (int i = 1; i < 256; i++) {
            final_max = (row_max[i] > final_max) ? row_max[i] : final_max;
        }
        
        // Fourth pass: find first row index of final_max
        int final_xidx = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            // Find first occurrence by scanning forward
            int found = (row_max[i] == final_max) && (final_xidx == 0);
            final_xidx = found ? i : final_xidx;
        }
        
        max = final_max;
        xindex = final_xidx;
        yindex = row_yindex[final_xidx];
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

```

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

float s13110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    int outer_iters = 100 * (iterations / 256);
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < outer_iters; nl++) {
        float row_max[256];
        int row_yindex[256];
        
        // First pass: find max and its first column index in each row
        // Vectorizable by using separate reductions
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float local_max = aa[i][0];
            int local_idx = 0;
            
            // Vectorizable max reduction
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current_val = aa[i][j];
                int is_greater = current_val > local_max;
                local_max = is_greater ? current_val : local_max;
                local_idx = is_greater ? j : local_idx;
            }
            
            // Handle equal values: find first occurrence
            // This small loop doesn't block vectorization of main reduction
            if (local_idx == 0) {
                for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                    if (aa[i][j] == local_max) {
                        local_idx = j;
                        break;
                    }
                }
            }
            
            row_max[i] = local_max;
            row_yindex[i] = local_idx;
        }
        
        // Second pass: find max across rows (fully vectorizable)
        float final_max = row_max[0];
        int max_row_idx = 0;
        
        for (int i = 1; i < 256; i++) {
            int is_greater = row_max[i] > final_max;
            final_max = is_greater ? row_max[i] : final_max;
            max_row_idx = is_greater ? i : max_row_idx;
        }
        
        // Handle equal values across rows
        if (max_row_idx == 0) {
            for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
                if (row_max[i] == final_max) {
                    max_row_idx = i;
                    break;
                }
            }
        }
        
        max = final_max;
        xindex = max_row_idx;
        yindex = row_yindex[max_row_idx];
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

Source code: 
```c
float s13110(int iterations,float aa[256][256])
{
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        max = aa[(0)][0];
        xindex = 0;
        yindex = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] > max) {
                    max = aa[i][j];
                    xindex = i;
                    yindex = j;
                }
            }
        }
        chksum = max + (float) xindex + (float) yindex;
    }
    return max + xindex+1 + yindex+1;
}
```

Optimized code:
```c

float s13110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    int outer_iters = 100 * (iterations / 256);
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < outer_iters; nl++) {
        float row_max[256];
        int row_yindex[256];
        
        // First pass: find max and its first column index in each row
        // Vectorizable by using separate reductions
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float local_max = aa[i][0];
            int local_idx = 0;
            
            // Vectorizable max reduction
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current_val = aa[i][j];
                int is_greater = current_val > local_max;
                local_max = is_greater ? current_val : local_max;
                local_idx = is_greater ? j : local_idx;
            }
            
            // Handle equal values: find first occurrence
            // This small loop doesn't block vectorization of main reduction
            if (local_idx == 0) {
                for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                    if (aa[i][j] == local_max) {
                        local_idx = j;
                        break;
                    }
                }
            }
            
            row_max[i] = local_max;
            row_yindex[i] = local_idx;
        }
        
        // Second pass: find max across rows (fully vectorizable)
        float final_max = row_max[0];
        int max_row_idx = 0;
        
        for (int i = 1; i < 256; i++) {
            int is_greater = row_max[i] > final_max;
            final_max = is_greater ? row_max[i] : final_max;
            max_row_idx = is_greater ? i : max_row_idx;
        }
        
        // Handle equal values across rows
        if (max_row_idx == 0) {
            for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
                if (row_max[i] == final_max) {
                    max_row_idx = i;
                    break;
                }
            }
        }
        
        max = final_max;
        xindex = max_row_idx;
        yindex = row_yindex[max_row_idx];
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

```

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

## FEEDBACK OUTPUT
Let’s go through the steps systematically.  

---

**1. Compilation errors in optimized code**  
The optimized code compiles (no syntax errors), so we proceed.

---

**2. Semantic comparison with simple test cases**  

The original code finds the **first occurrence** of the maximum element in row-major order (since it scans `i` from 0 to 255 and `j` from 0 to 255, and only updates `xindex, yindex` when `aa[i][j] > max`).  
The first occurrence means: if multiple positions have the same max value, the earliest in row-major order is chosen.

The optimized code does **two passes**:  
- First pass per row: finds max in each row and **first occurrence of that max within the row** (correct for per-row first occurrence).  
- Second pass across rows: finds max across rows and then **finds first row with that max** (correct for first occurrence in row-major order).  

But there’s a subtle bug:  
In the second pass, after finding `final_max` across rows, the code does:  

```c
for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
    if (row_max[i] == final_max) {
        max_row_idx = i;
        break;
    }
}
```

This correctly picks the first row with `final_max`. Then `yindex = row_yindex[max_row_idx]` is the first occurrence of max in that row. This matches original semantics.

However, the **first pass per row** already finds first occurrence of max in that row (the second inner loop after vectorizable reduction). That’s fine.

But wait — the **second pass across rows** first does a reduction to find `final_max` and `max_row_idx` (but this `max_row_idx` is the *last* row with max if multiple rows have same max, because of `is_greater ? i : max_row_idx` — it only updates when strictly greater, not when equal). Then it overwrites `max_row_idx` with the first row having that max. That’s correct.

So semantics should match.

Let’s test with a tiny example (2×2):  

Original:  
aa = [[3, 3], [3, 2]]  
Scan order: (0,0) max=3, x=0,y=0; (0,1) no update; (1,0) no update; (1,1) no update.  
Result: max=3, x=0, y=0.

Optimized:  
Row 0: max=3, first at j=0.  
Row 1: max=3, first at j=0.  
Across rows: final_max=3, first row with max is row 0 → x=0, y=0. ✅

Another example:  
aa = [[1, 2], [2, 1]]  
Original: (0,0) max=1,x=0,y=0; (0,1) max=2,x=0,y=1; (1,0) no update; (1,1) no update.  
Result: max=2, x=0, y=1.

Optimized:  
Row 0: max=2, first at j=1.  
Row 1: max=2, first at j=0.  
Across rows: final_max=2, first row with max is row 0 → x=0, y=1. ✅

So semantics match.

---

**3. Vectorization analysis**  

Compiler feedback says:  

- Loop at line 31 (finding first occurrence of max in row) not vectorized because could not determine number of iterations — but that loop is just scanning for equality, not a reduction; vectorization here is not critical.  
- Loop at line 22 (max reduction per row) not vectorized because “value that could not be identified as reduction is used outside the loop” — this is because `local_idx` is updated conditionally alongside `local_max`, breaking pure reduction pattern.  
- Loop at line 46 (max reduction across rows) same issue with `max_row_idx`.  

The problem: The compiler cannot vectorize the max-finding loops because they also track the index of the max, which is not a simple reduction (max of values) but a reduction with an associated index. This is a known pattern that compilers often fail to auto-vectorize.

---

**4. Optimization opportunity**  

Yes, there is an optimization opportunity:  
We can separate the max value reduction and index tracking to help vectorization.  
For example, find max value per row using vectorized max reduction, then in a separate pass find the first index of that max in the row.  
But the current code already does that (the second inner loop after the reduction). However, the first inner loop still tries to track index during max reduction — that’s unnecessary and prevents vectorization. We should remove that first inner loop’s index tracking entirely.

Specifically:

```c
float s13110(int iterations,float aa[256][256])
{
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        max = aa[(0)][0];
        xindex = 0;
        yindex = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] > max) {
                    max = aa[i][j];
                    xindex = i;
                    yindex = j;
                }
            }
        }
        chksum = max + (float) xindex + (float) yindex;
    }
    return max + xindex+1 + yindex+1;
}
```

Optimized code:
```c

float s13110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    int outer_iters = 100 * (iterations / 256);
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < outer_iters; nl++) {
        float row_max[256];
        int row_yindex[256];
        
        // First pass: find max and its column index in each row
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float local_max = aa[i][0];
            int local_idx = 0;
            
            // Vectorizable max reduction
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current_val = aa[i][j];
                int is_greater = current_val > local_max;
                local_max = is_greater ? current_val : local_max;
                local_idx = is_greater ? j : local_idx;
            }
            
            // Find first occurrence of max in this row
            // This maintains the original semantics
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] == local_max) {
                    local_idx = j;
                    break;
                }
            }
            
            row_max[i] = local_max;
            row_yindex[i] = local_idx;
        }
        
        // Second pass: find max across rows and its first occurrence
        float final_max = row_max[0];
        int max_row_idx = 0;
        
        for (int i = 1; i < 256; i++) {
            int is_greater = row_max[i] > final_max;
            final_max = is_greater ? row_max[i] : final_max;
            max_row_idx = is_greater ? i : max_row_idx;
        }
        
        // Find first row with the maximum value
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            if (row_max[i] == final_max) {
                max_row_idx = i;
                break;
            }
        }
        
        max = final_max;
        xindex = max_row_idx;
        yindex = row_yindex[max_row_idx];
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

```

---

**1. Compilation errors in optimized code**

**2. Semantic comparison with simple test cases**  

The original code finds the **first occurrence** of the maximum element in row-major order (since it scans `i` from 0 to 255 and `j` from 0 to 255, and only updates `xindex, yindex` when `aa[i][j] > max`).  
The first occurrence means: if multiple positions have the same max value, the earliest in row-major order is chosen.

The optimized code does **two passes**:  
- First pass per row: finds max in each row and **first occurrence of that max within the row** (correct for per-row first occurrence).  
- Second pass across rows: finds max across rows and then **finds first row with that max** (correct for first occurrence in row-major order).  

But there’s a subtle bug:  
In the second pass, after finding `final_max` across rows, the code does:  

```c
for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
    if (row_max[i] == final_max) {
        max_row_idx = i;
        break;
    }
}
```

This correctly picks the first row with `final_max`. Then `yindex = row_yindex[max_row_idx]` is the first occurrence of max in that row. This matches original semantics.

However, the **first pass per row** already finds first occurrence of max in that row (the second inner loop after vectorizable reduction). That’s fine.

But wait — the **second pass across rows** first does a reduction to find `final_max` and `max_row_idx` (but this `max_row_idx` is the *last* row with max if multiple rows have same max, because of `is_greater ? i : max_row_idx` — it only updates when strictly greater, not when equal). Then it overwrites `max_row_idx` with the first row having that max. That’s correct.

So semantics should match.

Let’s test with a tiny example (2×2):

Result: max=3, x=0, y=0.

Optimized:  
Row 0: max=3, first at j=0.  
Row 1: max=3, first at j=0.  
Across rows: final_max=3, first row with max is row 0 → x=0, y=0. ✅

Another example:  
aa = [[1, 2], [2, 1]]  
Original: (0,0) max=1,x=0,y=0; (0,1) max=2,x=0,y=1; (1,0) no update; (1,1) no update.  
Result: max=2, x=0, y=1.

Optimized:  
Row 0: max=2, first at j=1.  
Row 1: max=2, first at j=0.  
Across rows: final_max=2, first row with max is row 0 → x=0, y=1. ✅

So semantics match.

---

**3. Vectorization analysis**  

Compiler feedback says:  

- Loop at line 31 (finding first occurrence of max in row) not vectorized because could not determine number of iterations — but that loop is just scanning for equality, not a reduction; vectorization here is not critical.  
- Loop at line 22 (max reduction per row) not vectorized because “value that could not be identified as reduction is used outside the loop” — this is because `local_idx` is updated conditionally alongside `local_max`, breaking pure reduction pattern.  
- Loop at line 46 (max reduction across rows) same issue with `max_row_idx`.  

The problem: The compiler cannot vectorize the max-finding loops because they also track the index of the max, which is not a simple reduction (max of values) but a reduction with an associated index. This is a known pattern that compilers often fail to auto-vectorize.

---

**4. Optimization opportunity**  

Yes, there is an optimization opportunity:  
We can separate the max value reduction and index tracking to help vectorization.  
For example, find max value per row using vectorized max reduction, then in a separate pass find the first index of that max in the row.  
But the current code already does that (the second inner loop after the reduction). However, the first inner loop still tries to track index during max reduction — that’s unnecessary and prevents vectorization. We should remove that first inner loop’s index tracking entirely.

Specifically:  
In the per-row pass:  
1. Vectorized max reduction to find `row_max[i]`.  
2. Then find first `j` where `aa[i][j] == row_max[i]` for `row_yindex[i]`.  

Currently, step 1 is not vectorized because of the `local_idx` update. Remove `local_idx` from step 1 entirely.

---

**5. Conclusion**  

The optimized code matches semantics but is **not fully optimized** because the max reduction loops are not vectorized due to index tracking. Since there is an optimization opportunity (splitting max value and index finding completely), we should output **FAIL**.

**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.

    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

Optimized code:
```c

float s13110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    int outer_iters = 100 * (iterations / 256);
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < outer_iters; nl++) {
        float row_max[256];
        int row_yindex[256];
        
        // First pass: find max in each row (pure reduction, no index tracking)
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float local_max = aa[i][0];
            // Vectorizable max reduction
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current_val = aa[i][j];
                local_max = current_val > local_max ? current_val : local_max;
            }
            row_max[i] = local_max;
        }
        
        // Second pass: find first occurrence of max in each row
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            int local_idx = 0;
            float target_max = row_max[i];
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] == target_max) {
                    local_idx = j;
                    break;
                }
            }
            row_yindex[i] = local_idx;
        }
        
        // Third pass: find max across rows (pure reduction)
        float final_max = row_max[0];
        for (int i = 1; i < 256; i++) {
            final_max = row_max[i] > final_max ? row_max[i] : final_max;
        }
        
        // Fourth pass: find first row with max value
        int max_row_idx = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            if (row_max[i] == final_max) {
                max_row_idx = i;
                break;
            }
        }
        
        max = final_max;
        xindex = max_row_idx;
        yindex = row_yindex[max_row_idx];
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

```

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```
